I don’t have the exact number with me, but looking at it, I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be cleaner, more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your most recent actual weight and height, even approximately, from a recent doctor's visit or home scale?\n\nPatient: I don’t have the exact weight or height with me. I just know my weight has drifted lower than my doctor wants, and—even seeing that—I still think a slightly lower number would feel more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes where you eat a large amount of food and feel like you can't stop, or used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to compensate for eating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I haven’t thought of my eating in those terms.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as your periods becoming irregular or stopping altogether, since your weight has gone down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed any particular changes, but I don’t keep close track of it.\n\nDoctor: Even if you don't recall the exact number, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight in pounds or kilograms, and your height?\n\nPatient: I don't know the actual number, honestly. I just know it’s gone down more than my doctor would like. When I see the chart, though, I still think a little lower would feel more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Has your doctor specifically told you whether your current weight falls into the 'underweight' category for your height (such as a BMI below 18.5), or is it still within a normal weight range but just lower than before?\n\nPatient: My doctor has said it’s lower than they’d like. I don’t know whether they specifically called it underweight, though. When I see the numbers, I still feel like getting a little lower would be more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Even without knowing the exact number, can you tell me roughly how many pounds or kilograms you think you've lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I can’t give you a number—I haven’t been tracking it that closely. I just know it’s drifted down more than my doctor is comfortable with, although I still look at the chart and think a little lower would be more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical signs like feeling unusually cold, dizziness, hair thinning, or weakness since your weight went down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed anything specific like that, at least not enough that I connected it to my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your overall sense of self-worth or how you feel about yourself is heavily tied to your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know I’m not trying to look young or anything. But yes—how I feel about myself does depend a lot on whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. If they do, I feel like I’m handling myself better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in your clothing size, such as needing to go down two or more sizes, since your weight started decreasing?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of clothing sizes. I just know my weight has gone down more than my doctor would like, and I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be easier to manage.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I just know it’s drifted down more than my doctor is comfortable with, although I still look at the chart and think a little lower would be more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical signs like feeling unusually cold, dizziness, hair thinning, or weakness since your weight went down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed anything specific like that, at least not enough that I connected it to my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your overall sense of self-worth or how you feel about yourself is heavily tied to your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know I’m not trying to look young or anything. But yes—how I feel about myself does depend a lot on whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. If they do, I feel like I’m handling myself better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in your clothing size, such as needing to go down two or more sizes, since your weight started decreasing?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of clothing sizes. I just know my weight has gone down more than my doctor would like, and I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be easier to manage.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your height, even approximately, and the last actual weight number you saw on a scale or medical chart, even if it was months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the exact height or the last number, honestly. I just know my weight has drifted down more than my doctor likes. When I do see it in the chart, though, I still think a little lower would be a cleaner, more manageable number.\n\nDoctor: Since exact numbers are hard to recall, would you be willing to step on the scale here in the office right now so we can get an accurate current weight and calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I’d rather not right this second. I know my weight has drifted down more than my doctor likes, but when I see the number, I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be cleaner, more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to be weighed here in the office if we turn the display away so you don't see the number, just so I can get an accurate reading for your chart?\n\nPatient: Okay, if I don’t have to see the number, I can do that. I know it’s gone down more than my doctor wants, although part of me still thinks a lower number would be easier to manage.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I don’t know whether they specifically called it underweight, though. When I see the numbers, I still feel like getting a little lower would be more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Even without knowing the exact number, can you tell me roughly how many pounds or kilograms you think you've lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I can’t give you a number—I haven’t been tracking it that closely. I just know it’s drifted down more than my doctor is comfortable with, although I still look at the chart and think a little lower would be more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical signs like feeling unusually cold, dizziness, hair thinning, or weakness since your weight went down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed anything specific like that, at least not enough that I connected it to my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your overall sense of self-worth or how you feel about yourself is heavily tied to your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know I’m not trying to look young or anything. But yes—how I feel about myself does depend a lot on whether my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. If they do, I feel like I’m handling myself better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in your clothing size, such as needing to go down two or more sizes, since your weight started decreasing?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of clothing sizes. I just know my weight has gone down more than my doctor would like, and I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be easier to manage.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your height, even approximately, and the last actual weight number you saw on a scale or medical chart, even if it was months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t remember the exact height or the last number, honestly. I just know my weight has drifted down more than my doctor likes. When I do see it in the chart, though, I still think a little lower would be a cleaner, more manageable number.\n\nDoctor: Since exact numbers are hard to recall, would you be willing to step on the scale here in the office right now so we can get an accurate current weight and calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I’d rather not right this second. I know my weight has drifted down more than my doctor likes, but when I see the number, I still catch myself thinking a little lower would be cleaner, more manageable.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to be weighed here in the office if we turn the display away so you don't see the number, just so I can get an accurate reading for your chart?\n\nPatient: Okay, if I don’t have to see the number, I can do that. I know it’s gone down more than my doctor wants, although part of me still thinks a lower number would be easier to manage.\n\nDoctor: Now that you're on the scale, let's get your height measured too—can you step over to the height marker so I can record both and calculate your BMI for your chart?\n\nPatient: Yes, I can do that. I know the weight has gone down more than my doctor is comfortable with. It’s just hard not to look at the chart and think that a slightly lower number would feel more manageable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
